- [x] **B20** — **DONE cycle 47, QA PASS after 1 retry.** 10 nullable KYC columns added to `users` (`database/migrations/011_kyc_profile_fields.sql`): guarantor name/phone/relationship/address, collateral description, ID type+number, next-of-kin name/phone/relationship. (Career/job reuses the existing `occupation` column — no duplicate added.) `profile.update` (member self-service) and `members.update` (admin edit, writes a second `UPDATE users` for any subset of the 10 keys) both accept them as fully optional — never required, always skippable, admin can override any subset. `complete_profile.html`/`complete_profile_sql.js` add an optional "Guarantor & Next of Kin" section; `manage_members_new_sql.js` adds a collapsed `<details>` "KYC details (optional)" disclosure in the admin edit modal, styled in `styles/pages.css` (Japandi tokens, 44×44 touch target, AA contrast) by ui-designer. **QA first pass FAILED:** `list_members()` never returned the 10 KYC columns, so the admin disclosure always rendered blank regardless of saved data. **Fix required care beyond the obvious patch:** `list_members()` is callable by plain `member`-role callers (it powers the ordinary roster view), so naively adding the KYC columns to its SELECT would have leaked every member's ID number/collateral/next-of-kin contact info laterally to every other member — caught before dispatch, not by QA. Fixed by branching `list_members()` on the caller's own role (`require_role()`'s return value, previously discarded): `admin`/`senior_admin`/`treasurer` callers get a `JOIN users` variant with the 10 KYC columns; `member`-role callers get the byte-identical pre-existing SELECT, unchanged. ## CYCLE 74 (2026-07-22) — OWNER DIRECTIVE: "take out the inline JS so we standardise everything throughout the app. Also for the SPA side navmenu do not reload it. Only show a new modern loader within the page container and no need to reload nav menu." No PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box reopens; cycles not renumbered.

**Survey done this cycle (coordinator, grep across every page):**
- **SPA loader:** confirmed via `scripts/spa-router.js:394-450` (`navigateTo()`) — sidebar/topbar/mobile-nav are ALREADY never rebuilt per navigation (cycle 56 architecture: only `updateActiveNav()` runs, no `initNav` re-call) — that half of the owner's ask is already satisfied by the existing design, nothing to change there. But there IS a real gap: between the `fetch()` + `waitForStylesheets()` await (:403-440) and `currentContent.replaceWith(newContent)` (:442), there is currently ZERO visual feedback — the OLD page's content just sits static for however long the fetch+stylesheet-wait takes, which can read as an unresponsive click. This is the fix target: a loader shown ONLY inside `.dashboard-content`/`#mainContent`, never touching the persistent nav.
- **Inline JS inventory (grep, every `pages/*.html` + root):** two distinct patterns to standardize/remove —
  1. **The 3-line `initNav()` bootstrap block** — `<script type="module">import {initNav} from '../scripts/nav_sql.js'; initNav({variant, activePage, pageTitle});</script>` — appears near-identically on ~19 of the 22 authenticated pages (confirmed on admin_dashboard, analytics, seed_money_overview, financial_reports, and 15 others via grep; `accept_invitation.html`/`login.html`/public pages correctly have none, they don't call initNav). This is the single most repeated inline-JS pattern in the app — a prime standardization target.
  2. **7 pages with a bare (non-module) `<script>` block carrying real inline logic**, never extracted to an external file: `index.html:2706` (nav-scroll-effect + feature-row IntersectionObserver — real landing-page behavior), `pages/admin_dashboard.html:1905` (chart-bar animation + a `addSystemAlert()` demo/placeholder function — needs inspection, may be dead demo code), `pages/admin_registration.html:1189` (password-strength calculator, real logic, feeds the `.strength-bar` elements from cycle 73), `pages/user_analytics.html:381` (uninspected), `pages/analytics.html:225`, `pages/financial_reports.html:133`, `pages/seed_money_overview.html:140` (these 3 were flagged in cycle 72 as "duplicate tab-click logic, redundant but harmless" riding alongside the real module-script tab handling — likely DELETE candidates, not extract, pending each specialist's own verification that the module script already covers the same behavior).

- **Audit 1 (backend):** `api/lib/penalty.php` full read + line-by-line comparison against `api/handlers/payments.php`'s `compute_contribution_penalty()` (the "same engine shape" claim payments.php:125 makes but was never verified) — confirm both use identical grace-period/fixed-daily/rounding rules or document the actual divergence. Plus a fast re-confirm sweep of `api/lib/money.php`/`loans.php`/`repayments.php`/`payments.php`/`cycle.php` for any drift since cycle 62 (git-log-free static check: still 100% integer-minor, still routes through money.php, percentage-penalty refusal still intact).
- **Audit 2 (frontend):** (a) the ~19 client-side aggregation call sites cycle 62 left in place — for each, determine concretely whether JS floating-point addition of the specific decimal strings involved can produce a cent-level display error (not just "theoretically safe," actually reason about magnitude/precision, or construct a concrete counterexample); (b) grep cycles 64-74's changed files (`manage_loans_sql.js`'s overdue predicate, `user_dashboard_sql.js`'s `isLoanDue`, all table-conversion `createXRow()` functions, the 6 newly-extracted scripts) for any new arithmetic on a money value or any reintroduced local `formatCurrency`-family definition — confirm `scripts/utils_financial.js` is still the sole source app-wide.

### CYCLE 75 — AUDIT RESULTS

**Audit 1 (backend) — money math CLEAN, one real functional gap found.** Zero new ad-hoc money arithmetic anywhere in `money.php`/`loans.php`/`repayments.php`/`payments.php`/`cycle.php` (grep for `round(`/`number_format(`/`floatval(`/`(float)` across all four handlers: 0 hits). `compute_loan_schedule()` confirmed still the sole interest source. Percentage-penalty refusal confirmed intact on BOTH engines (`penalty.php:117-119` and `payments.php:187-189`), still a hard throw, never a guessed formula.
`api/lib/penalty.php` vs `payments.php`'s `compute_contribution_penalty()` — the accrual formula IS identical (grace-period clamp, day-diff, whole-day normalization, daily-rate product, all via `money.php` helpers, verified line-by-line). **But the "same engine shape" claim (payments.php:125) is FALSE for the settlement half:** `compute_loan_penalty` nets off `penalty_settlements` (paid+waived) before returning outstanding (`penalty.php:190-211`); `compute_contribution_penalty` has NO equivalent netting — it never queries `penalty_settlements` at all. The table's own schema (`database/migrations/005_fixed_daily_penalties.sql:53-55,81-82`) explicitly reserves a `paymentId` column alongside `loanId` ("exactly one of these is set"), clearly designed to support this — but `penalty_settlements` is grep-confirmed to ONLY ever be written with `loanId` (`repayments.php:575,795`), never `paymentId`. **Practical effect:** a contribution penalty can never be explicitly waived or recorded as settled the way a loan penalty can; it simply recomputes live from arrears each time, and once arrears clear the whole struct silently zeroes (`payments.php:177`) with no settlement record ever created. **This is a real, owner-decision-needed gap — see BLOCKED table below, not silently built.**

**Audit 2 (frontend) — verdict: no patch needed on precision; one real reintroduced-formatter bug found and fixed.** Float-precision risk on the ~19 remaining client-side summation sites: CONCRETELY assessed, not just assumed — split into Pattern A (`toMinor`, integer-cent summation, zero risk, exact up to 2^53) and Pattern B (`numberOf`/`parseFloat` float summation, worst-case error bound calculated at ~4.4×10⁻⁷ MWK for 200 rows at MWK 10M scale, ~11,000× below the 0.005 threshold needed to flip a displayed cent) — every Pattern-B site confirmed to route through `formatCurrency` before display, which re-rounds away the negligible float noise. **Prior cycles' judgment was correct — now proven, not just reasoned.** `isLoanDue`/Overdue predicates and all 12 `createXRow()` table functions (cycles 65/71/72) confirmed to do only threshold checks/display formatting, zero new money arithmetic (one non-money progress-bar percentage noted at `manage_loans_sql.js:572`, not a violation). 5 of cycle 74's 6 newly-extracted scripts confirmed to touch zero money fields. **`scripts/registration-password-strength.js:276` reintroduced a local `formatCurrency` (0dp) instead of importing the shared module** — genuinely new, introduced during cycle 74's inline-script extraction (the extraction was verbatim from the ORIGINAL inline script, which had never been migrated to the shared formatter back when cycle 63 did its app-wide sweep, since it was inline at the time and invisible to that grep). Dispatched for the same mechanical fix as cycle 63's Wave 1/2.

**Fix — DONE, QA PASS.** `scripts/registration-password-strength.js` now imports `formatCurrency` from `./utils_financial.js`; local 0dp definition deleted (not commented out); all 3 call sites (textContent assignments) untouched, resolve correctly to the 2dp shared formatter (same intentional 0dp→2dp shift as cycle 63's other migrations, not a regression). **A real second issue surfaced by the fix and corrected in the same dispatch:** the file now has a top-level ES `import`, but its `<script>` tag in `pages/admin_registration.html:1189` was a classic (non-module) script — would have thrown a SyntaxError and broken the entire registration page. Fixed by adding `type="module"` to that tag. QA independently verified: script execution order preserved (still runs before the adjacent `registration_sql.js` module script), no code in the file depended on classic-script synchronous timing (it only registers event listeners / grabs already-parsed DOM elements), and — the real risk of a classic→module conversion — confirmed strict-mode compatibility: all variables (`strengthText`/`strengthColor`/`rulesFile`) are properly `let`-declared before assignment, no implicit globals, no `with`, no duplicate params. `node --check` clean, diff confined to exactly the import/deletion + the one `type="module"` attribute. **DONE.**

## CYCLE 75 COMPLETE — money logic cross-checked, one real bug patched, one real gap escalated
**Backend:** zero ad-hoc money math found anywhere (money.php/loans.php/repayments.php/payments.php/cycle.php/penalty.php all clean), percentage-penalty refusal still intact on both engines. One genuine functional gap found and recorded as **B23** (contribution penalties have no settlement/waiver ledger, unlike loans) — this needs the owner's product decision, not a guessed fix, so it's parked in the Blocked table rather than built.
**Frontend:** the float-precision concern carried since cycle 62 was concretely proven safe (not just assumed) — worst-case error is ~11,000× below the threshold needed to flip a displayed cent, and every summation site re-rounds through the shared formatter before display anyway. A genuine regression WAS found and fixed: cycle 74's inline-script extraction had accidentally reintroduced a local currency formatter (missed by cycle 63's original sweep because it was still inline at the time) — now consolidated onto the shared module, with a second real bug (a script-tag/module-loading mismatch the fix itself exposed) caught and corrected in the same pass before it could break the registration page.

## CYCLE 76 (2026-07-22) — OWNER DIRECTIVE: "build the contribution-penalty waiver endpoint, then confirm the loader doesn't refresh the sidebar — sidebar stays static, no loader on that side." No PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box reopens; cycles not renumbered.

**Part 1 — B23 waiver endpoint: BUILT.** See B23 entry (Blocked table, below) for the full implementation record — resolved this cycle, moved out of Blocked.

**Part 2 — sidebar/loader scoping: ALREADY CORRECT, verified not patched.** Direct code inspection (coordinator, not delegated — this re-verified cycle 74's own shipped work rather than building anything new) of `scripts/spa-router.js` confirms both owner requirements were already true:
- The loader (`showLoader()`/`hideLoader()`, cycle 74) is invoked ONLY against `document.querySelector("#mainContent .dashboard-content")` (line 562) and only ever appends/removes its overlay element as a child of that same node — it has no reference to the sidebar anywhere in its code.
- `navigateTo()` never calls `initNav()`/`renderAdminNav()`/`renderUserNav()` on any navigation (grep-confirmed zero occurrences) — only the lightweight `updateActiveNav()` runs, which moves the active-link highlight and topbar title in place. The sidebar/mobile-nav/toast-container are explicitly named in `SHARED_BODY_CHROME_SELECTOR` (`#mainContent, #sidebar, #sidebarOverlay, .mobile-nav, #toastContainer, .top-nav, #mobileMenuOverlay, #mobileMenu`) and `isSyncablePageBodyElement()` skips anything matching it — so even the body-level content-sync pass (which handles modals/overlays) cannot touch the sidebar.
- No code change was needed or made to `spa-router.js`/`nav_sql.js` for this part — confirmed via `git diff --stat` that neither file appears in this cycle's diff.

**OWNER FOLLOW-UP (same cycle):** "the loader is putting opacity over the sidebar every time I change to a different page" — a real, currently-observed symptom, contradicting the static-analysis confirmation above. Re-investigated rather than dismissed.

**Root cause found: NOT a scoping bug in the loader/router code (re-confirmed correct by direct inspection — `.dashboard-content` has `position:relative` (`styles/admin-layout.css:508-511`), the sidebar is `position:fixed` and a DOM sibling of `#mainContent`, not a descendant, and no other CSS rule anywhere overrides `.main-content`'s `position:relative` (admin_dashboard.html's page-local `<style>` duplicate only repeats `margin-left`/`min-height`, never touches `position`) — the overlay is structurally incapable of painting over the sidebar's box.** The real cause: **`scripts/nav_sql.js` and `scripts/spa-router.js` — the two files that carry ALL of this session's router/loader fixes across cycles 56/57/66/74 — are the ONLY two ES modules in the entire app loaded via a plain, unversioned `import` specifier.** Cycle 74's cache-busting (`?v=20260722` on every page's `<script src>` tag) deliberately and correctly did NOT touch these, because at the time nothing about them had just changed — but this cycle's own confirmation pass edited neither, so a browser that cached `nav_sql.js`/`spa-router.js` from BEFORE any of the loader-scoping work (cycles 56-74) landed would still be running an old copy today, and no page-level cache-bust can invalidate a module fetched via a bare `import` specifier. This is the exact same stale-script bug class the settings.html investigation (cycle 66) first surfaced in this app — just on a shared module this time instead of a per-page one.

**Fix:** added the same `?v=20260722` version query param to the import SPECIFIER (not a `<script src>` tag — these are `import`/dynamic-`import()` statements) at all 4 places `nav_sql.js`/`spa-router.js` are referenced, keeping the string byte-identical everywhere so the browser's module registry resolves them all to the SAME singleton instance rather than accidentally double-instantiating either module: `scripts/manage_rules_sql.js:21`, `scripts/page-bootstrap.js:32`, `scripts/spa-router.js:616` (the dynamic `import()` inside `navigateTo()`), and `scripts/nav_sql.js:22` (its own static import of `spa-router.js`). Confirmed via repo-wide grep these are the ONLY 4 import sites of either file — no HTML page imports either module directly. `node --check` clean on all 4 files.

**OWNER FOLLOW-UP #2 (same cycle):** "still dimming after a hard refresh — shows a blue dark screen when switching pages. Instead create a loader modern money like fancy that will just be central in the middle section of every admin page when switching, then enhance UI."

**REAL ROOT CAUSE FOUND (this was not a caching issue at all — the cache-busting fix in follow-up #1 above was reasonable hygiene but not the actual bug):** every dashboard page's own `init()` calls `showSpinner(true)` (e.g. `scripts/admin_dashboard_sql.js:84,1501-1506`) at the start of its OWN data load, toggling `.hidden` on a `<div class="spinner-overlay" id="spinner">` element. That element's CSS (`styles/design-system.css`'s base `.spinner-overlay` rule) is `position:fixed;inset:0;background:rgba(10,22,40,0.9)` — i.e. `--bn-primary` (deep navy-blue) at 90% opacity, covering the ENTIRE viewport. Since cycle 56's SPA router re-runs every page's `init()` on every in-app navigation (not just first load), this full-viewport dark overlay now fires on EVERY page switch, covering the sidebar too — exactly the "blue dark screen dimming the sidebar" reported. The markup itself (`#spinner`, sibling of `#mainContent`/`.dashboard-content` in `<body>`) is identical on all 19 SPA-converted pages, confirmed by direct inspection, not assumed.

**Fix — redesigned + rescoped, mechanical + design work, not a one-line patch:**
1. `styles/design-system.css` — new "CONTENT-SCOPED LOADER" rule block added (kept the ORIGINAL full-viewport dark rule completely untouched, since it's still correct for the 3 standalone pages with no dashboard shell — accept_invitation/admin_registration/complete_profile — confirmed via grep they have zero `.dashboard-content`). New descendant selector `.dashboard-content > .spinner-overlay` overrides `position:fixed`→`absolute` (scoped to `.dashboard-content`, which already has `position:relative`), swaps the dark navy background for the app's own light `--glass-bg` glass token, and replaces the plain ring spinner with a "spinning gold coin" (`.dashboard-content > .spinner-overlay .spinner`, `background:var(--bn-gradient-accent)`, `box-shadow:var(--bn-shadow-glow)`, a `rotateY` flip animation, a centered "K" glyph in navy via `::after`) inside a soft white card (`box-shadow:var(--bn-shadow-xl)`, pop-in animation). Zero new colors invented — every value reused from existing `--bn-*`/`--glass-bg` tokens. Brace-balance-checked (300/300) since no CSS linter was available in this environment.
2. Markup relocation across all 19 SPA-whitelisted pages (13 admin + 6 user) — the SAME `#spinner`/`#loadingOverlay` element (unchanged id/class/contents) moved from being a `<body>`-level sibling (after `</main>`) to being the FIRST CHILD of `.dashboard-content`, so the new descendant-selector CSS actually applies. Zero JS changed anywhere — `showSpinner()`/`hideSpinner()` on every page still just toggles `.hidden` on `getElementById(...)`, unaffected by the element's new DOM position. Dispatched as 2 parallel frontend-specialist batches (10 + 9 pages, file-disjoint). **QA PASS** — verified across all 20 files (19 HTML + CSS): original full-viewport `.spinner-overlay` base rule byte-identical/untouched (still correct for the 3 standalone non-dashboard pages, confirmed absent from this diff entirely), new rules correctly use the `.dashboard-content > .spinner-overlay` CHILD combinator throughout (not a loose descendant selector), spot-checked 5 files including the money-critical `manage_loans.html`/`manage_payments.html` — spinner now the true first child of `.dashboard-content`, inner markup byte-identical, div-tag-balance unchanged, zero JS touched. **DONE.**

**Coin-loader design polish — DONE, QA PASS.** Refined the first-pass coin loader into an actually convincing "coin flip": tighter perspective + `scaleX`/shadow dimming at the edge-on points of the rotation so it genuinely reads as thinning/flipping rather than squishing; the glyph independently foreshortens/fades in sync. Composition pulled into the same glassy visual family as the SPA-router's own navigation loader (`--glass-bg`/`--glass-border`/blur instead of an opaque white card). Added a `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback (a real accessibility gap the first pass missed — a perpetually-flipping coin is genuinely uncomfortable for motion-sensitive users) that swaps the 3D flip for a calm opacity pulse. QA independently confirmed the original base full-viewport spinner rule (still used by the 3 standalone pages) is untouched, every new selector stays properly child-combinator-scoped to `.dashboard-content`, all colors reuse existing tokens (zero new hardcoded values), and the reduced-motion media query genuinely overrides rather than layers onto the default animation. **DONE.**

## CYCLE 76 COMPLETE — waiver endpoint built, sidebar/loader scoping re-confirmed correct + a real stale-module-cache bug fixed + the real "blue dark screen" bug found, fixed, and redesigned
Every part of this cycle's asks is closed: the contribution-penalty waiver endpoint is built and verified; the loader/sidebar interaction was traced to a real bug (every page's own data-load spinner, not the SPA nav-transition loader, was the actual full-viewport dark overlay dimming the sidebar on every switch) — fixed by rescoping + redesigning it into a confined, modern gold coin-flip loader across all 19 dashboard pages, then given a genuine design-polish pass (coin realism, glass composition, motion accessibility). The earlier stale-module-cache fix (nav_sql.js/spa-router.js versioning) remains in place as good hygiene, though it was not the actual cause of this particular symptom.

**Part 3 — "then enhance UI" (owner's trailing instruction).** Scope decision, made without re-asking per standing instruction: the app already received a full 25-page UI/UX pass in cycle 73 (visual hierarchy, spacing, empty/loading states, table consistency). Re-running that same full sweep again so soon, with no new area named, would be redundant churn. Given this whole cycle's context is the admin dashboard experience (the loader fix), the enhancement is scoped there: a premium-polish pass on the 13 admin pages, pushing them toward the same "modern, fancy" feel the new coin loader established — visual hierarchy, spacing rhythm, hover/interaction states, card/stat-tile consistency — beyond what cycle 73's accessibility-focused pass covered. If the owner wants the same depth applied to the 6 user-facing pages or a specific named area next, that's a fast follow-up, not re-derived from scratch.

**Part 3 — DONE.** `styles/design-system.css`'s `.card`/`.stat-card`/`.btn-primary` were already at the target polish level (confirmed — nothing to redo there). Gaps found in the SHARED SHELL files every one of the 13 pages actually loads: `styles/admin-layout.css`'s `.content-section`/`.page-stat-card`/`.dashboard-content .page-stat` had NO hover states at all (static, inconsistent with the rest of the design system); `styles/pages.css`'s `.content-section` hover didn't step up a shadow level like its sibling `.card`/`.info-card` rules do. Added consistent hover-lift/shadow-step treatments + tighter numeral letter-spacing on stat values, reusing only existing tokens — fixing the shared shell fixes it on all 13 pages at once, the highest-leverage move available. **Environment note:** the `Grep` tool was down (`EPERM` spawning ripgrep) for part of this dispatch, limiting how many per-page CSS files/inline `<style>` blocks got individually audited — the specialist correctly prioritized the two universal shell files over guessing at per-page overrides blind. Coordinator followed up directly (via `Bash grep`, which was unaffected) on the 3 flagged files (`manage_members.css`/`analytics.css`/`settings.css`): confirmed all 3 are **DEAD legacy stylesheets** — zero `<link>` reference to any of them in the entire app (their pages fully switched to `design-system.css`'s `.table.table-responsive` component back in cycle 71's table conversion) — so no follow-up fix was needed there; flagging them as a future cleanup/deletion candidate (needs owner sign-off per the safety rail, same as every other dead-file finding this project has logged).

**QA's first pass on the shell CSS FAILED with a real finding:** `.content-section` is defined in BOTH `admin-layout.css` and `pages.css` at identical specificity, and 11 pages load both (admin-layout.css first) — the two rules' `transition` shorthand declarations weren't additive, so `pages.css`'s (loading later) silently clobbered `admin-layout.css`'s new border-color-hover transition, making it snap instead of ease. Fixed with a one-line change: `pages.css`'s transition list expanded to match `admin-layout.css`'s exactly (`box-shadow` + `border-color`), so the two rules no longer fight regardless of load order. The two files' base/hover box-shadow VALUES still legitimately differ (a pre-existing divergence, not part of this defect — each file's own hover step is internally consistent) — flagged as a future design-consolidation candidate, not fixed now. Re-QA PASS confirmed the transition lines are now byte-identical and no other file was touched. **Part 3 DONE.**

## CYCLE 78 (2026-07-22) — OWNER DIRECTIVE: "fix the UI/UX on tablet size and mobile — most cards' content is going over the cards on most pages." No PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box reopens; cycles not renumbered.

**ROOT CAUSE FOUND (coordinator investigation, before any dispatch) — two-part, both confirmed by direct code read, not assumed:**
1. `styles/design-system.css:14-26` — a pre-existing (pre-dates this build-loop entirely, committed by the owner directly on 2026-01-18, commit `3b1c38b`) global rule forces `overflow: visible !important` on `.content-section`/`.content-card`/`.rules-container`/`.info-card`/`.member-card`/`.interest-rates-display`/`.content-section-body`/`.content-card-body` at ≤768px, with a comment: "prevent border-radius from hiding content." This DEFEATS each of those classes' own base `overflow: hidden` rule (confirmed present on `.content-card` at `pages/admin_dashboard.html:604`, `.member-card` at `pages/contacts.html:59`, `.rules-container` at `pages/view_rules.html:23`) — so instead of clipping, overflowing content now visibly spills past the card border at mobile/tablet widths. **`.interest-rates-display` is a dead class reference in this rule — not defined anywhere in the current app, harmless.**
2. Confirmed WHY content overflows in the first place: `.content-card`/`.member-card`'s header rows (`.content-card-header`, `.member-card-header`) and `.info-card-header` (shared, `styles/pages.css:438-443`) are `display:flex; justify-content:space-between` with a text title on one side and an icon/badge/action-link on the other — and NONE of them set `min-width:0` on the text child. Flex children default to `min-width:auto`, so a long title (a member name, a payment description, a loan number) refuses to shrink or wrap and pushes the row wider than the card. Combined with finding 1's disabled clipping, that overflow is now VISIBLE — exactly "cards' content going over the cards."

**Where these classes actually live (grep-confirmed, so scope is precise, not "most pages" guesswork):** `.content-section` is shared-only (`admin-layout.css`/`pages.css`, already hover-polished cycle 76, not itself the culprit here). `.info-card` is shared (`pages.css:424-468`, used across ~11 admin + several user pages — highest-leverage single fix). `.content-card` is LOCAL to `pages/admin_dashboard.html` and `pages/user_dashboard.html` (each with its own copy, redefined again inside several of their own breakpoint media queries — multiple `.content-card-header` redeclarations confirmed in `user_dashboard.html` at ~1021/1105/1617, needs careful per-breakpoint reading). `.member-card` is local to `pages/contacts.html` and `pages/user_dashboard.html`. `.rules-container` is local to `pages/view_rules.html`.

**Fix strategy — do NOT touch the blanket `overflow:visible!important` safety-net rule itself** (removing it risks reintroducing whatever the original 2026-01-18 border-radius/clipping bug was, and it's genuinely unclear from the code alone what that was). Instead, eliminate the actual overflow CAUSE: add `min-width: 0` to the text-bearing flex child, `overflow-wrap: break-word`/`word-break: break-word` to the title/value classes themselves, and `flex-shrink: 0` to the fixed-size sibling (icon/badge/button) so it holds its size while the text shrinks/wraps — the standard, correct fix for this exact flexbox overflow pattern. If a title+action pairing genuinely can't fit side-by-side even after shrinking at the narrowest breakpoints, allow `flex-wrap: wrap` there as a fallback so they stack vertically instead of overflowing horizontally.

### CYCLE 78 dispatch plan (2 parallel, file-disjoint)
- Dispatch 1: `styles/pages.css` (`.info-card-header`/`.info-card-title`, shared — highest leverage, fixes it on every admin+user page that uses `.info-card` at once) + `pages/view_rules.html` (`.rules-container`, local).
- Dispatch 2: `pages/admin_dashboard.html` + `pages/user_dashboard.html` (`.content-card-header`/`.content-card-title`, EVERY breakpoint redeclaration in each file, plus `user_dashboard.html`'s own `.member-card`) + `pages/contacts.html` (`.member-card-header`).

- `pages/view_rules.html`: discovered the REAL flex header is `.rules-header` (not `.rules-container`, which has no flex at all) — fixed `.rules-title` + the `.download-btn` sibling found there.

**OBJECTIVE:** `pages/messages.html`'s `#messagesList` shipped with a static placeholder that is itself an `.empty-state` block — same `📬` icon and same styling `messages_sql.js:316` renders for a genuinely empty inbox — reading "Loading messages...". So before data arrives, and on any slow or failed load, the page looks identical to "you have no messages." That is exactly the silent-blank-screen confusion E4 exists to prevent. Chosen over the other unblocked follow-ups (per-page `.tab` CSS duplication, `#applyPenaltyModal` orphan markup, `admin-layout.css`'s dead `.user-menu-dropdown` block, `user_dashboard.html`'s remaining inline styles) because it is the only one with a real user-visible correctness effect — the rest are pure maintainability debt.

**Fix:** replace that placeholder with a real loading indicator built from the page's OWN already-loaded classes (`.spinner` + `.loading-text`, both already present in this file's `#spinner` overlay block at :310-315), so the loading state is visually distinct from the empty state. Static HTML only, one file, one region — no JS touched (`messages_sql.js` clears and re-renders `#messagesList` at runtime and never reads the placeholder's contents).

**CYCLE 80 — DONE, QA PASS.** The build step swapped the markup as briefed — **but the coordinator caught that change as a REGRESSION before it reached QA**, and the polish step fixed it: the base `.spinner`/`.loading-text` classes are authored for the DARK full-viewport overlay (`.loading-text { color: var(--bn-white) }`, `.spinner { border: 3px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.15) }`), so used bare on the WHITE messages card they would have rendered **white-on-white — invisible**, strictly worse than the misleading-but-visible state they replaced. This is the SAME bug class already found on `user_analytics.html` in cycle 77 (dark-context styles landing on a light surface) — third occurrence of this pattern in the codebase, worth watching for. Fixed with page-scoped overrides in `messages.html`'s own inline `<style>`: `#messagesList > .spinner` (light `--bn-gray-lighter` track + `--bn-accent` top-color, 32px — deliberately smaller than the 48px full-page spinner since this is an in-list indicator, `align-self:center` because `.messages-list` is a flex column that would otherwise left-align/stretch it) and `#messagesList > .loading-text` (`--bn-gray`), plus a `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback reusing the existing global `bn-coin-pulse` keyframe.
**QA verified the two things that actually matter:** (1) the new loading state is genuinely distinguishable from BOTH runtime states — it read `messages_sql.js:206-211` (error → `emptyState("❌", …)`) and `:312-317` (empty → `emptyState("📬", …)`), confirming both call `list.textContent = ""` first so they fully replace the placeholder rather than layering onto it, and the new spinner+text has no icon glyph or `.empty-state` structure to confuse with either; (2) the fix actually renders visibly — specificity checked properly rather than assumed (`#messagesList > .spinner` is (1,1,0) vs the base `.spinner`'s (0,1,0), and the inline `<style>` also loads after the `design-system.css` link, so the cascade wins on both counts). All tokens real, scope confined to the one file, `#messagesList` JS hook intact, tags balanced. **DONE.**

**Part 3 — Money Masters rulebook read + loan-interest group setting VERIFIED CORRECT.** Read `Money Masters Saving Rules and Regulations.pdf` (3 pages) — the real business rules the app must honour. Key group settings it defines: seed MWK 1,000,000 (due 5 Jan); **loan interest 15% first month + 15% on the carried/reducing balance** each subsequent month; repayment period **2 months for loans < MWK 500,000, 3 months for loans > MWK 500,000**; **MWK 5,000/day late penalty** after the 5th (in addition to interest); 30-member cap; 11-month cycle (share-out by 10 Dec, last compulsory loan Oct); equitable forced-loan distribution of idle funds; ≥1 guarantor + collateral + next-of-kin.
**Verified the loan-interest setting end-to-end by executing the REAL engine** (`api/lib/money.php`'s `compute_loan_schedule()`) against Money Masters inputs (`month1/2/3 = 15/15/15`, reducing balance — the rates a Money Masters group configures via `rules.update`). A throwaway PHP harness (scratch only, deleted after) ran 3 rulebook scenarios; every figure matched the rulebook by hand:
  - MWK 300,000 / 2 mo (below-500k rule): M1 interest 45,000 (15% of 300k), M2 22,500 (15% of carried 150k), total interest 67,500, repay 367,500. ✓
  - MWK 900,000 / 3 mo (above-500k rule): M1 135,000, M2 90,000 (of carried 600k), M3 45,000 (of carried 300k), total 270,000, repay 1,170,000. ✓
  - MWK 1,000,000 / 3 mo: M1 150,000, M2 100,000, M3 50,000, total 300,000, repay 1,300,000; principal reconciles to 1,000,000 exactly. ✓
  **Result: ALL CHECKS PASS — the loan-interest group setting produces exactly the interest the Money Masters rulebook specifies** (15% first month + 15% on the reducing carried balance), for both the below-500k and above-500k repayment-period rules, with the schedule reconciling to the cent. The engine is fed per-group rates from `group_rules.loanInterestRateMonth1/2/3` (a configurable group setting via `rules.update`), so any group — Money Masters included — sets its own rates and the engine applies them correctly. (The app's schema *default* is 10/7/5 declining, not Money Masters' flat 15/15/15 — that's fine, it's a per-group setting each group configures; the point proven is the ENGINE honours whatever the group sets, exactly per the rulebook.)
  **Rulebook-vs-app gaps NOTED for a follow-up review (not built this step — recorded so the planner can triage which, if any, are real):** the MWK 5,000/day penalty maps to `loanPenaltyDailyAmount` (fixed-daily engine, verified working M2.5) — a Money Masters group sets it to 5000; the 30-member cap, the 11-month cycle bound, the ≥2-guarantees-per-person limit, and the rollover-as-new-loan rule (rulebook g) are rules whose enforcement-vs-app-state should be spot-checked against `group_rules`/handlers in a dedicated review cycle.

## CYCLE 83 (2026-07-22) — Money Masters rule-enforcement: the 30-member cap. Traces to **B1** (group + membership integrity) and directly serves the owner's "works amazing logic-wise" directive. The planner chose direction B (rulebook-vs-app enforcement check) over a responsive audit — cheaper, fully static, fits flaky subagent capacity, and a preliminary grep already smelled a real gap. Cycles not renumbered.

**AUDIT (coordinator-direct — scout was queued but the check was cheap enough to run inline) → CONFIRMED GAP.** The Money Masters rulebook: "The group will have a maximum of 30 members." The app enforced this NOWHERE:
- No `maxMembers` (or any member-cap) column in any migration. `group_statistics.totalMembers` (001:140) is a derived stat cache, not a cap. The `loanRulesMax*` columns are loan limits, unrelated.
- 4 `INSERT INTO members` paths: `groups.php:109` (group creator — member #1, correctly needs no cap), `members.php:113` (`members.add`), `members.php:558` (`members.create`), `invitations.php:51` `insert_member_from_user` (shared by invite-accept `:198` + code-redeem `:432`). **None of the three non-creator paths did any headcount check** — the only `COUNT(*)`s in members.php (:197/:426/:639) are the last-senior-admin guard, not a total-member cap. A group could grow unbounded past 30, silently violating the rulebook.

**FIX (coordinator-applied — backend-specialist has a stale Firebase-era role def that refuses PHP; project convention is coordinator-applies + qa-reviews):**
- New `api/lib/membership.php`: `GROUP_MAX_MEMBERS = 30` constant (the rulebook's flat value) + `group_current_member_count(pdo, groupId)` (counts `status <> 'inactive'` — active+suspended occupy a slot, a deactivated/departed member frees theirs so a group can backfill) + `assert_group_has_capacity(pdo, groupId)` (409 if count ≥ cap). All defs `defined()`/`function_exists()`-guarded per the codebase's universal pattern.
- Gated all three non-creator join paths: the check sits at the top of `insert_member_from_user` (one call covers both invite-accept and code-redeem), after the duplicate guard in `members.add` (so re-adding says "already a member", not "group full"), and before the transaction in `members.create` (so a full group never creates an orphan user row). Group-create stays exempt (0→1 can't exceed 30).
- **Design choice (assumption recorded, ambiguous≠blocked): hardcoded constant 30, NOT a `group_rules.maxMembers` column.** The rulebook states a flat 30 for every group, and hardcoding avoids a live migration + the ordering hazard of shipping a gate that reads a column that may not exist yet. Making the cap a configurable per-group setting is logged as a **future enhancement** (needs a migration adding `group_rules.maxMembers INT DEFAULT 30`), not required by the rulebook.
- **Accepted limitation (documented in the code):** a count-then-insert race could momentarily reach 31 on truly-simultaneous joins to the same group (no `FOR UPDATE` lock). For a 30-person village group this is not a real-world concern and self-corrects; a lock can close it fully if ever needed.
- **Coordinator-verified before QA:** `php -l` clean on all 3 files; the full include chain (both handlers pulling membership.php twice + index.php loading both) boot-tested with NO redeclaration fatal (`GROUP_MAX_MEMBERS`=30, both helpers resolve); `$groupId` confirmed in scope + server-derived (not raw request body) at every call site. `qa-auditor` dispatched for the independent review.
- **Also recorded — the OTHER logged rulebook rules still unverified** (candidates for future enforcement cycles): the 11-month cycle bound, "a person can guarantee max 2 people", rollover-as-new-loan (rulebook g), and confirming a Money Masters group's `loanPenaltyDailyAmount` is set to the rulebook's MWK 5,000/day.

**CYCLE 83 — QA PASS. DONE.** `qa-auditor` independently confirmed: all three non-creator join paths gated (invite-accept + code-redeem via `insert_member_from_user`, `members.add`, `members.create`), group-create correctly exempt (grep found no other `INSERT INTO members`); `$groupId` at every gated call site is server-authorized (`require_role` for the admin paths, a DB-row `groupId` for the invitation paths — never a raw client body value that could dodge the check); ordering correct at both members.php sites (duplicate-guard before cap in add; cap before transaction in create so no orphan user); the `defined()`/`function_exists()` guards prevent any redeclaration fatal across the double-include; prepared statement + hardcoded `status` literal (no injection); `php -l` clean; zero unrelated edits. The design choices (hardcoded 30, active+suspended count, disclosed count-then-insert race) all assessed sound for a 30-person village group. **The Money Masters 30-member cap is now enforced app-wide.**
- **Guarantor rule note (found while triaging):** "a person can guarantee max 2 people" (rulebook, Guarantors §c) CANNOT be enforced today — the app stores guarantor info as free-text KYC profile fields (`guarantorName`/`Phone`/`Relationship`), NOT a structured member→member guarantee relationship. Enforcing the 2-per-person limit would need a real guarantor-link table + assignment flow (a feature, not a gate). Logged as a FUTURE IDEA, not a quick fix.

### CYCLE 83 — DISPATCH BRIEF (codebase-scout, READ-ONLY)
```
AGENT      codebase-scout
OBJECTIVE  Determine whether the app enforces the Money Masters 30-member-per-group cap on ANY path that adds a person to a group, and whether a configurable member-cap group setting exists at all. FINDINGS ONLY — change no file. Return CONFIRMED-ENFORCED (with the file:line of the count-vs-cap check) or GAP (no cap check on ≥1 join path, name each unguarded path).
FILES      Read only these, trace every member-INSERT path:
             api/handlers/invitations.php — insert_member_from_user (~:51), respond_invitation accept branch (~:187), redeem_invitation_code (~:361)
             api/handlers/members.php — any create/add/invite action that INSERTs into members (and note the existing COUNT(*) at ~:197/:426/:639: what each guards)
             api/handlers/approve_registrations.php (if it exists) — does approving a registration INSERT a member into a group?
             api/handlers/groups.php — group creation/join, if it adds the creator as a member
             database/migrations/*.sql — does group_rules (or any table) define a maxMembers / member-limit / cap column? Grep the schema.
CONTEXT    - Money Masters rulebook (read cycle 82): a group is capped at 30 members. The app stores per-group settings in the group_rules table, applied server-side (money engine already reads loanInterestRateMonth1/2/3, loanPenaltyDailyAmount from it — proven correct cycle 82).
           - Members live in the `members` table keyed by (groupId, uid) with a `role` and `status` ('active' etc.). A member is added by: invitation-accept, invitation-code redeem, possibly a direct admin add, possibly registration-approval, and the group creator at group creation.
           - Planner's preliminary grep found NO maxMembers column in migrations and NO headcount-vs-cap check in members.php — the scout CONFIRMS this properly and checks the OTHER join paths (invitations/approvals/group-create) the planner did not fully trace.
           - This is the SQL/PHP app (Firebase is deleted, D2). Server-side enforcement is the only real gate; a client-side check would not count.
DO NOT     - Do NOT edit any file (findings only). Do NOT run destructive SQL or hit the live DB — static read + grep of the repo only.
           - Do NOT audit the responsive/UI layer, other rulebook rules (11-month cycle, guarantor limit, rollover, penalty amount), or anything beyond the member-cap question — those are separate future cycles.
           - Do NOT assume the planner's preliminary grep is complete — verify it and extend to every join path listed.
ACCEPT     Returns, with file:line evidence:
             (1) Whether a configurable member-cap setting/column exists anywhere (group_rules or elsewhere) — yes+where, or no.
             (2) For EACH member-INSERT path (invitation-accept, code-redeem, direct-add, registration-approval, group-create): does it count current active members and reject at/over the cap? file:line of the check, or "NO cap check" naming the unguarded insert.
             (3) A one-line verdict: CONFIRMED-ENFORCED, or GAP (list the unguarded paths and whether the cap value even exists to enforce).
COMPLEXITY standard — read-only static audit of ~4-5 handler files + the schema; no money arithmetic, no live DB, no multi-file edit.
```

Remaining unverified rulebook rules (11-month cycle bound, ≥2-guarantees-per-person, rollover-as-new-loan, penalty default) stay queued for later spot-check cycles. ## CYCLE 84 (2026-07-22) — OWNER DIRECTIVE: "information in the admin & user portals is all over the place — hard to understand once a user logs in. Review and fix to make life easier. Also for modals check table sizes; if cramped on smaller screens display them better with bordered tables or cards." Traces to **E4/C6** (the post-login portal surfaces); no PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN box reopens. Cycles not renumbered.

**Grounding (coordinator, before dispatch — the actual current information architecture, read not assumed):**
- **Admin dashboard (`pages/admin_dashboard.html`)** body order today: System Alerts → **Quick Actions (8 cards)** → **Due Payments This Month** → **Stats Grid (Collections / Active Loans / Pending / Arrears)** → Content Grid (Collection Trends chart + Pending Approvals). **THE PROBLEM: the at-a-glance summary numbers — the single most important "how is my group doing" glance — are buried at position 4**, below the action grid AND a detail list. Standard dashboard IA leads with KPIs, then primary actions, then detail feeds. The current order is inverted, which is exactly "all over the place / hard to understand on login."
- **User dashboard (`pages/user_dashboard.html`)** body order: **Quick Actions** → Notifications → Payment Calendar → Upcoming Payments, with a `.hero-stats` block (the member's key figures) whose placement needs confirming. Leading with Quick Actions before the member sees their own standing (what they've paid, what's due, active loans) is the same inverted priority.
- **Modals:** the page tables are already `.table-responsive` (collapse to cards ≤768px via `design-system.css`); `styles/mobile-modals.css` (imported by design-system.css) already handles some modal widths at breakpoints. The owner still finds tables/modals cramped on small screens → the responsive treatment needs strengthening (bordered/card fallback, modal body width, no horizontal cram).

**FIX — 2 file-disjoint dispatches, parallel-safe:**
- **Dispatch 1 (frontend-specialist) — dashboard information architecture, `pages/admin_dashboard.html` + `pages/user_dashboard.html`.** Reorder sections to a clear priority hierarchy (admin: Stats FIRST → Quick Actions → Due Payments → Trends/Approvals; user: member's standing/hero-stats FIRST → what's due/upcoming → actions → notifications). Structural reorder + grouping + spacing ONLY — every element `id`/`class`/`data-*` preserved byte-for-byte (the `_sql.js` scripts inject data by id; reordering blocks is safe since `getElementById` is order-independent, but a renamed/dropped id breaks the page). No content, no logic, no JS change.
- **Dispatch 2 (ui-designer) — modal + table responsiveness, shared stylesheets `styles/mobile-modals.css` + `styles/design-system.css` (`.table-responsive`/modal rules) ONLY.** Strengthen the small-screen treatment so tables and modal bodies don't cram: ensure `.table-responsive` card-collapse reads cleanly inside a modal, modals fill available width on ≤480/≤768px without horizontal overflow, and cramped tabular content falls back to bordered cards. Shared-CSS only (no dashboard HTML), so file-disjoint from Dispatch 1.

**CYCLE 84 — DONE (coordinator-verified; QA subagent deferred — subagents failed 3× this session on a usage limit that resets ~23:40 local, so the coordinator did thorough inline verification, same pattern as cycle 81).**

**Dispatch 1 (dashboard IA reorder) — the specialist COMPLETED both files before its session-limit death (its last streamed line said "reading user_dashboard" but the git diff shows both fully reordered).** Coordinator then verified it about as rigorously as possible WITHOUT a browser:
- **`admin_dashboard.html`** new order: `#systemAlerts` → **Stats Grid (moved up — the 4 KPIs now greet the admin)** → Quick Actions → Due Payments → Content Grid. Exactly the target priority.
- **`user_dashboard.html`** new order: **`.hero-stats` (member's standing, now leads)** → Upcoming Payments → Payment Calendar → Quick Actions → Notifications (passive, last).
- **PROVEN SAFE — the reorder cannot have broken the data-injection wiring:** compared the full `id=""` set and the `class=""` multiset of each file BEFORE (`git show HEAD:`) vs AFTER — **byte-identical for both files** (id set: same names + same counts, zero added/dropped/renamed; class multiset md5 identical). Since `_sql.js` injects by `getElementById` (order-independent) and every id is provably preserved, the reorder is structurally guaranteed not to break rendering. Confirmed it's a PURE block reorder via md5 of sorted-line content: the ONLY non-reorder change is ONE `style="margin-bottom: var(--bn-space-6)"` added per file (to the moved `.stats-grid` / `.content-grid`, matching the spacing the other sections already carry — token-based, no magic number). Tag balance identical before/after (admin 51/51 divs, user 144/144).

**Dispatch 2 (modal/table responsiveness) — the ui-designer NEVER applied any change (session-limit death before editing; CSS files were clean in git). Coordinator did the highest-signal part inline.** Found + fixed a REAL latent bug: `mobile-modals.css`'s generic "Tables in Modals" rules force `.modal-content table tr { display:table !important }`, which **OVERRIDES and BREAKS** the `.table-responsive` card-collapse (from `design-system.css`) for any responsive table shown INSIDE a modal — i.e. a responsive table in a modal was forced into a cramped horizontal-scroll table instead of clean cards, exactly the owner's "cramped tables in modals" complaint. Added a scoped `@media (max-width:768px)` block re-asserting the card collapse for `.modal-content .table-responsive` only (thead hidden, rows→block, `overflow-x:visible`), so responsive tables in modals now collapse to the same clean bordered cards page tables use. Tokens/existing-classes only; brace balance 58/58; the rest of `mobile-modals.css` (already comprehensive — full-width modals ≤480px, sticky header/footer, single-column form grids, iOS-zoom guard, safe-area insets) was correct and left untouched. The broader `.table-responsive` page-card treatment in design-system.css was assessed already-good (each row → white bordered card with radius/padding/shadow) and deliberately NOT churned.

**Honest limitation recorded:** neither change is pixel-verified — no browser in this environment. The reorder is structurally proven safe (id/class-identical) and the modal-table fix is a targeted, brace-balanced, token-only override, but the VISUAL result ("does it actually read better / look uncramped") wants a human eyeball or a live browser session. A `qa-auditor` diff review + ideally a Playwright pass on a live/local instance should confirm when subagent capacity returns.

## CYCLE 85 (2026-07-22) — OWNER (frustrated, legitimately): "portal cards stacked up / hard to know what's important; on the USER portal I can't find where to make monthly payments, pay arrears/upcoming, no buttons next to missed contributions, I CAN'T UPLOAD PROOF OF PAYMENT — almost everything is broken." Traces to **C5**/**M3.2**. CORE-WORKFLOW gap, NOT cosmetics. Cycles not renumbered.

**ROOT CAUSE — fully traced in code (coordinator; subagents rate-limited until ~23:40 local):**
The member CONTRIBUTION-payment workflow does not exist in the UI, though the backend is fully ready:
1. **`payments.record` allows member self-pay** — gates on `require_role($groupId, PAYMENT_ANY_MEMBER_ROLES)`, `targetUid` defaults to the caller. `files.upload` accepts member uploads. Backend is NOT the problem.
2. **No member UI wires to it** — only `manage_payments_sql.js` (ADMIN) calls `payments.record`. `loan_payments.html` handles member LOAN repayments only (that flow works). No member path to pay a contribution / arrears / missed month or upload contribution proof.
3. **THE TWIST — the member write-UIs ALREADY EXIST fully in `user_dashboard.html` markup but were NEVER WIRED.** `user_dashboard_sql.js`'s own header: it's a "READ/DISPLAY port" that deferred "the write flows ... out of this brief's scope, so those buttons surface a toast." Sitting dead in the markup:
   - **`#paymentModal`/`#paymentUploadForm`** (~:2109): a COMPLETE contribution-payment form (group, payment-type [seed_money/monthly_contribution/loan_repayment/penalty], amount, date, is-advance, **proof file**, submit). Unwired — "Upload Payment" just redirects to the loans page.
   - **`#loanModal`/`#loanRequestForm`** (~:2021): a COMPLETE "Book a Loan" form (group/amount/period/target-month/purpose/live interest calc/submit). Unwired — "Request Loan" toasts "not available", though `loans.request` is live and the rulebook §c has members book loans by the 15th.
   - Obligations/upcoming/arrears rows are DISPLAY-ONLY — no per-row Pay button.
The owner's experience is exact: the page LOOKS complete but the SQL migration never connected the member write-flows. "Almost everything is broken" = can see what's owed, cannot act on any of it.

**THE FIX (WIRING existing UI to ready backend, not a from-scratch build):**
- **P1 — wire contribution payment:** "Upload Payment" OPENS `#paymentModal` (not redirect); populate `#paymentGroup`; on submit `uploadProof(#paymentProof)` (copy the PROVEN `loan_payments_sql.js:535` helper verbatim) → `payments.record`. **Type-mapping care:** modal offers seed_money/monthly_contribution/loan_repayment/penalty but `payments.record` accepts only seed_money/monthly_contribution/service_fee, and monthly_contribution REQUIRES `month`. So: add a month selector (monthly only), add service_fee, add a `paymentMethod` field (required, missing from the modal), route loan_repayment → `loan_payments.html`, handle/drop penalty (no direct endpoint). 
- **P2 — per-obligation "Pay" buttons** on upcoming/arrears/missed rows that open `#paymentModal` pre-filled.
- **P3 — wire `#loanModal`** → `loans.request` (confirm with owner that member loan-booking is enabled per rulebook §c; this resolves the earlier "no member loan-request flow" gap).

**P1 BUILT (coordinator, inline — owner said "build it"; subagents rate-limited). node --check clean on both files.** The member contribution-payment flow is now wired end-to-end:
- **`pages/user_dashboard.html` `#paymentModal`:** fixed the payment-type options to the 3 the server accepts (seed_money / monthly_contribution / service_fee; dropped the invalid loan_repayment/penalty, added a note linking loan repayments to `loan_payments.html`); **added the required Month selector** (`#paymentMonth`, shown only for monthly_contribution); **added the required Payment Method selector** (`#paymentMethod`: cash/bank_transfer/mobile_money — the server requires it and the modal had none); added an optional Notes field; removed the cosmetic Payment Date field (server uses NOW()). All 13 ids verified unique, tag balance 146/146.
- **`scripts/user_dashboard_sql.js`:** imported `apiPost`; changed the "Upload Payment" button from a redirect to `openPaymentModal()`; wired close-button + overlay-click-close + payment-type-change (show/hide month) + form submit. New `handlePaymentSubmit()` validates every required field, uploads the proof, then `apiPost("payments.record", {groupId, paymentType, amount, paymentMethod, proofOfPaymentImageUrl, proofOfPaymentFileName, proofOfPaymentFileSize, [month, isAdvancedPayment], [notes]})` — no `targetUid`, so the server records it as the member's OWN pending claim. On success: close, reset, success toast, reload dashboard. New `uploadProof()` + `openPaymentModal()`/`closePaymentModal()` helpers. Uses `danger`/`success` toast types (confirmed colored in the CSS), not the uncolored `error`.

**SECOND REAL BUG FOUND + FIXED while building this — proof upload was broken for LOANS too.** `files.upload` returns the standard `{ok:true, data:{url,...}}` envelope, but `loan_payments_sql.js`'s `uploadProof` read `body.url` (top-level → always `undefined`), so it would ALWAYS throw "Upload did not return a file URL" — i.e. members could never upload loan-repayment proof either (matches the owner's "I can't upload proof of payments," which was true on BOTH paths). The new contribution `uploadProof` reads `body.data.url` correctly; also fixed `loan_payments_sql.js:566-569` the same way (`body.data.url` with a flat fallback). Both `node --check` clean. **This was almost certainly never caught because QA was static/diff review — a real file upload against the live envelope was never exercised.**

**P2 BUILT (coordinator, inline; owner: "do the pay now and any other pending functions"). node --check clean.** Per-obligation **"Pay" buttons** now sit on every upcoming-payment row (`buildUpcomingRow`) AND every arrears-modal row (added an "Action" column). Each opens `#paymentModal` pre-filled via `openPaymentModal({paymentType, month, amount})` — new `serverPaymentType(label)` maps the display label → the server enum (Seed Money→seed_money, Monthly Contribution→monthly_contribution + its month, Service Fee→service_fee), and `cleanAmount()` strips formatting so the number input accepts the owed amount. Arrears-row Pay closes the arrears modal first, then opens the payment modal. So a member taps "Pay" next to a specific missed month and the form is ready to submit — exactly the owner's "buttons next to contributions missed."

**P3 BUILT — the "Book a Loan" form is now wired.** `requestLoanBtn` (was a dead "not available" toast) now opens `#loanModal`; `openLoanModal()` populates the group select + the target-month select (was `required` but empty, which blocked submit); `handleLoanSubmit()` validates and POSTs `loans.request {groupId, principalAmount, repaymentPeriod, purpose}` (purpose = the dropdown + optional description) → lands PENDING for admin approval → success toast + dashboard reload. Close-button + overlay-click wired. Member loan-booking is IN the product model per rulebook §c ("members express loan requirements by the 15th"). This resolves the long-standing "no member-facing loan-request flow" gap (2026-07-14 maintenance note).

**BONUS — a 4th dead button fixed.** `viewPaymentDetailsBtn` ("Payment Details" quick action) had NO handler, and `showAllPaymentsModal()` (renders all verified payments) existed but nothing ever opened it — now wired together. After this the user dashboard has **ZERO dead/stub buttons** except two that genuinely need NEW backend endpoints (member↔member direct messaging = B21; ticket reply = no endpoint) and correctly toast that they're unavailable.

**CYCLE 85 SUMMARY — the member portal's core write-workflows, dead since the SQL migration, are now all wired:** pay any contribution (seed/monthly/service) with proof upload, from the quick action OR a per-obligation Pay button; request a loan; view all verified payments. Plus the `files.upload` envelope bug (`body.url`→`body.data.url`) that broke proof upload on BOTH the loan and contribution paths is fixed. Files touched: `pages/user_dashboard.html` (payment modal markup), `scripts/user_dashboard_sql.js` (all wiring), `scripts/loan_payments_sql.js` (upload-envelope fix). All `node --check` clean; all ids unique; tag balance 146/146.

**VERIFICATION GAP (important, unchanged):** every one of these is money-adjacent and was NOT browser-tested (no browser in this environment). The wiring is correct against each server contract, mirrors the (now-fixed) proven upload pattern, lints clean, and every referenced function/id is confirmed to exist — but a real click-through on a live/local instance (open payment modal → monthly + month + proof → submit → admin sees a pending claim; request a loan → admin sees a pending loan; upload proof actually stores a file) MUST confirm before trusting for live money. Run `qa-auditor` + a Playwright pass when subagent capacity returns / the app is running.

## CYCLE 86 (2026-07-22) — LIVE BROWSER E2E VERIFICATION of the cycle-85 member write-flows (owner ran `php -S 0.0.0.0:8000` locally; coordinator drove a real Chromium via Playwright MCP). This is the browser verification cycle 85 flagged as required. Cycles not renumbered.

**METHOD:** Registered a fresh admin + group via the 5-step wizard (`[PAY TEST - safe to delete] Payment Flow Group`, seed 50k, monthly 10k, **fixed** penalty 500/day — chose fixed not percentage to avoid the known percentage-penalty 501); created a plain member via `members.create`; logged in as the member; drove the real UI.

**VERIFIED WORKING (real browser, real DB):**
- **Dashboard IA reorder (cycle 84):** confirmed live — the member dashboard leads with the hero stats (their standing), then Upcoming Payments, then actions. Reads clearly.
- **Per-obligation "Pay" buttons (cycle 85 P2):** confirmed rendering on every overdue Upcoming-Payment row. Clicking "Pay" on the January row opened `#paymentModal` **correctly pre-filled**: group selected, Payment Type = Monthly Contribution, **Month = January (the new selector I added, shown only for monthly)**, **Payment Method present (new field)**, Amount pre-filled to the owed **10,000.00**. Exactly as designed.
- **Contribution payment RECORD half (cycle 85 P1):** `payments.record` with my exact payload shape returned **201** — a real PENDING claim (paymentType monthly_contribution, month January, amount 10,000, method cash, notes, the member's own uid via self-pay, approvalStatus `pending`). Payload contract 100% correct.
- **Loan request (cycle 85 P3):** the loan modal opened + populated (group + the target-month selector I added, which was `required`-but-empty before and blocked submit); the form submitted the correct `loans.request` payload; error handling confirmed live (a config-driven 422 surfaced as a red toast to the member); and after the loanNumber fix below, `loans.request` returned **201** (LN-0001, pending).
- **Full member→admin loop:** logged back in as admin — `payments.list` and `loans.list` both show the member's submissions as PENDING, ready to approve. The workflow is closed.

**TWO REAL BUGS FOUND + FIXED by the live test (neither is in cycle-85's new code — both pre-existing, invisible until the flow was actually exercised):**
1. **Proof upload was reading the wrong response level (`body.url` vs `body.data.url`)** — already caught + fixed in cycle 85 on BOTH the contribution (new) and loan (`loan_payments_sql.js`) paths. Live test re-confirmed the upload now sends correct multipart and reaches `files.upload`.
2. **`loans.loanNumber` had a GLOBAL unique constraint but every handler generates it PER-GROUP** (`LN-` + count-in-this-group + 1), so the first loan in every group is `LN-0001`. The DB already had `LN-0001`/`LN-0002` from an earlier group, so this member's first loan request collided on the global unique → masked **500**. This broke loan creation for every group after the first — never caught because the member loan-request flow was only just wired + exercised. **FIX: migration `012_loan_number_per_group_unique.sql`** (idempotent, non-destructive) drops the global `uq_loans_loanNumber` and adds composite `uq_loans_group_loanNumber (groupId, loanNumber)` — matches the per-group intent (the handler's own comment wrongly claimed loanNumber was "not unique-constrained"; corrected the comment too). **Applied live to the DB** (dropped global, added composite — verified); loan request then returned 201. `php -l` clean.

**ONE ENVIRONMENT GAP (not a code bug) — proof upload can't be tested on THIS dev server:** `files.upload` correctly returns 422 "File verification is unavailable on the server" — the fail-closed guard for a missing `fileinfo`/`finfo` PHP extension (confirmed: `extension_loaded('fileinfo')===false` in `C:\tools\php83\php.ini`). The app is RIGHT to refuse (it can't MIME-sniff an upload safely without fileinfo). **To test the full proof-upload path locally the owner must enable `fileinfo`:** uncomment `extension=fileinfo` in `C:\tools\php83\php.ini` and restart `php -S`. On the production cPanel host fileinfo is normally enabled, so uploads should work there. Everything downstream of the upload (record with proof URL) is proven working.

**Test data left in the live DB (clearly named, safe to delete):** group `[PAY TEST - safe to delete] Payment Flow Group` (id `2060a3098044f1d71a70708b49e71595`), admin `paytest1784000001@example.com` / `TestPass123`, member `paymember1784000002@example.com` / temp pass `b8b2e98c97fe541f`, one pending payment + one pending loan (LN-0001). The group's `loanRulesMaxLoanAmount` was set to 500,000 during testing (was 0 — I hadn't filled it in the wizard).

**NET: cycle 85's member write-flows are verified working end-to-end in a real browser** (payment modal + Pay buttons + record + loan request + admin visibility), with the only untested step being the actual file byte-upload, blocked solely by the dev server's missing `fileinfo` extension — and one genuine pre-existing production bug (loan-number collision) found and fixed along the way.

## CYCLE 87 (2026-07-22) — OWNER (live review of localhost): (1) the "Advanced Payment" checkbox shows even for an already-DUE payment (wrong — advanced = paying ahead, not settling arrears); (2) user-dashboard `.dashboard-content` list sections aren't tables on desktop despite having space. Reviewed + fixed both, verified live in a real browser.

**FIX 1 — Advanced-payment logic.** `#isAdvancedPayment` was always visible. An advance is only valid when paying a FUTURE (not-yet-due) monthly contribution — never for a due/overdue one. Fix: `#advancedPaymentGroup` (new id on the checkbox's form-group) is hidden by default; new `updateAdvancedPaymentVisibility()` shows it ONLY when `paymentType === monthly_contribution` AND the selected month's obligation due date is strictly in the future (`isFutureContributionMonth()` reads `window.__dashboardData.obligations.monthlyContributions.months` and compares dueDate to today); otherwise hides it and force-unchecks. Wired to paymentType change, a new paymentMonth change listener, and openPaymentModal. **Verified live:** Pay on an overdue Jan row → advanced group `display:none`; select Sept (due 2026-09-30, future) → `display:block`; select Jan (due 2026-01-31, past) → `display:none`.

**FIX 2 — Upcoming Payments → responsive table.** `renderUpcomingPayments`/`buildUpcomingRow` built `.list-item` divs (a card list at every width). Replaced with the app's `.table.table-responsive` component via a shared `buildUpcomingTable(items)` + `createUpcomingTableRow(item)` (columns Payment / Due Date / Amount / Status / Action, each `<td>` with a `data-label` for the mobile card-collapse; the Pay button lives in the Action cell). The "Upcoming Payments" MODAL (`openUpcomingPaymentsModal`) now uses the same shared builder (removing the dangling `buildUpcomingRow` reference). **Verified live:** desktop (>768) shows a real 5-column table (8 rows), no `.list-item` left; at ≤768 it collapses to stacked cards (thead `display:none`, cells `display:flex`, `data-label` shown via `::before`, no horizontal scroll).

**Other `.dashboard-content` sections reviewed, left as-is (correctly non-tabular):** Notifications (a feed), Payment Calendar (visual), Quick Actions (button grid), hero stats (stat cards). The arrears + all-payments modals were already tables (cycle 85/prior). Upcoming Payments was the one genuine list-that-should-be-a-table.

Files: `pages/user_dashboard.html` (advanced-group id + hidden default), `scripts/user_dashboard_sql.js` (both fixes). `node --check` clean; ids unique; tag balance 146/146. Test data (`[REVIEW TEST - safe to delete] Layout Group` + member) created for the live check and then deleted from the DB.

**ROOT CAUSE of the "member in a group" gap:** the **"Group Members" hero stat (`#totalMembers`) was NEVER populated** — grep confirmed ZERO JS references to that id, and the dashboard's data-load (`payments.obligations` + `payments.list` + `loans.list`) never fetched `members.list` at all. So the count sat at the hardcoded markup value "0" forever.

- Group Members: **"4 Members"** (was 0) ✓
- Loans: **1** with details "Received MWK 30,000.00 / Balance MWK 30,000.00" ✓

- Upcoming Payments table + Pay buttons render ✓
`members.list` reliability checked (5/5 sequential + 5/5 concurrent = 200); a single earlier 500 was a transient `php -S` blip under heavy multi-request test load, not a code bug.

**Minor remaining (NOT fixed — low priority, needs a backend change):** the multi-group chooser overlay's per-group member count uses `group.statistics.totalMembers`, which `groups.mine` doesn't return, so it shows 0 there. Only appears when a member belongs to multiple groups and must pick one; the main dashboard stat is fixed. Fixing it would need `groups.mine` to return a per-group member count (an N+1 today).

Files: `scripts/user_dashboard_sql.js` (uncommitted). Test data (`[STAT TEST - safe to delete] Accounting Group` + 3 members) deleted from the DB after the check.

## CYCLE 89 (2026-07-22) — OWNER: "make sure we don't have the same mistakes in the admin dashboard — not just members, but amounts and other things." Systematic review of the admin dashboard (and a cross-scan of all admin pages) for the "stat never populated / wrong data source" bug class, verified live.

**SCAN (all admin pages, grep every data-display id vs its paired `_sql.js`):** the admin dashboard's core tiles are all referenced (unlike the user dashboard's `#totalMembers` which had 0 refs). Flagged-but-not-bugs: `manage_members`'s member-detail fields (memberCareer/guarantor/ID/etc.) are 0-ref because `members.list` doesn't return them — the documented **B20** KYC gap, not new; `manage_payments`'s `#applyPenaltyModal` + interest/penalty fields are the intentionally-orphaned dead modal (cycle 70). No new "never populated" stat found on the admin pages.

**REAL BUG FOUND + FIXED — the admin dashboard "Arrears" tile was drastically WRONG.** Verified live (group of 3 members, one approved payment, one loan): the tile showed **MWK 0.00** while the group genuinely owed **MWK 1,379,000** (confirmed by summing every member's `payments.obligations`). Root cause: the tile used `payments.list` → `summary.totalArrears`, which only sums the `arrears` column of RECORDED payment ROWS. An obligation a member simply never paid has NO row, so it was invisible — the admin saw ~0 and would think the group was paid up. This is the same *class* the owner worried about (a figure not pulling the real data), on the amounts side.
**FIX (server-side, respecting A2's no-client-money-math rule):** new endpoint **`payments.groupArrears`** (GET, admin-only) — `group_arrears_summary()` in `payments.php` computes group-wide outstanding arrears + penalties by running the EXACT same per-obligation math as `my_obligations()` (rules − verified-paid, plus `payment_penalty_or_501`'s `amountOutstanding`) across every ACTIVE member, all in integer minor units, so the group total reconciles with the sum of each member's own dashboard. `admin_dashboard_sql.js` now fetches it in the load and the Arrears tile reads `groupArrears.totalArrears` (falling back to the old recorded-row summary only if the endpoint fails). **Verified live:** endpoint returns totalArrears 1,379,000.00 (arrears 500,000 + penalties 879,000) — exact match to the obligations ground truth — and the tile now displays **MWK 1,379,000.00** (was 0). `php -l` + `node --check` clean.

**Also confirmed correct on the admin dashboard (live):** Collections (`summary.verifiedCollected` = the approved payments) ✓, Active Loans count ✓, Pending Approvals ✓, Due Payments ✓, Pending Approvals list ✓. (An earlier "Active Loans: 0" in testing was a transient `php -S` blip on `loans.force`, not a bug — reproduced 201 immediately after.)

**Minor follow-up NOT done (noted):** the admin dashboard's "Collection Trends" PIE CHART (`admin_dashboard_sql.js:345`) still derives its Arrears SLICE from recorded-row `p.arrears`, so that one visualization understates the same way the tile did — the headline tile (the number an admin reads first) is fixed; the chart slice is a secondary, lower-priority consistency follow-up.

Files: `api/handlers/payments.php` (+`group_arrears_summary`), `api/index.php` (+route), `scripts/admin_dashboard_sql.js` (fetch + tile). Test group `[ADMIN STAT TEST - safe to delete]` deleted from the DB after the check.

## CYCLE 90 (2026-07-23) — OWNER DIRECTIVE (verbatim): "fix logic like when a member is requesting a loan show their current standing like any current loans, how that will look like, have admin be able to also control such conditions, things like taking double loans before finishing the other or things like whether a loan can be given even without paying outstanding arrears, penalties, etc. Be calculative."

This is a **NEW FEATURE** (loan-eligibility standing + server-side gating + admin-configurable conditions), not polish on an existing box. It does not trace to any existing A1–F deliverable, so it is **owner-promoted** into scope as a new deliverable group **G (Loan eligibility & standing)** — added below the PROJECT COMPLETE WHEN checklist by owner directive via `/build-loop` (same authorization pattern as the owner-directed cycles 72–89). COMPLEXITY: high (money arithmetic + server-side gate + rules schema change).

### G. Loan eligibility & standing (owner-promoted cycle 90)
- [x] **G1** — Server-computed **loan standing / eligibility** via GET `loans.eligibility` (loans.php `loan_eligibility_endpoint`): returns active loans + remaining balance, outstanding arrears + penalties (minor units), and an optional server-priced preview (principal/interest/total/schedule via compute_loan_schedule, period+principal clamped to group rules). Rendered in the member loan modal (#loanStandingPanel + server-fed calc summary, no client money math). **LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-07-23**: well-formed standing + reduced-balance preview (10% → interest 10000 / total 60000 on 50000/3mo).
- [x] **G2** — Server-side **eligibility ENFORCEMENT inside `request_loan`** via shared `loan_eligibility_check()` (same code path as the endpoint, single source of truth): blocks when activeLoanCount ≥ maxActiveLoans, or arrears>0 with requireArrearsCleared, or penalties>0 with requirePenaltiesCleared — re-checked after require_role(); force_loan (admin override) exempt. **LIVE-VERIFIED**: 2nd request while 1st pending → 409 "maximum number of active loans"; 1st → 201. **ALL THREE GATES LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-07-23**: arrears gate → 409 "Outstanding arrears of 50000.00 must be cleared…"; penalty gate (isolated, arrears gate off) → 409 "Outstanding penalties of 1564000.00 must be cleared…".
- [x] **G3** — **Admin controls** (backend live-verified; UI QA PASS): group-rules toggles editable in the Loan Settings modal (manage_loans.html/`.js` — #maxActiveLoans + #requireArrearsClearedBeforeLoan / #requirePenaltiesClearedBeforeLoan switches, via `LOAN_SETTINGS_FIELD_MAP` + `handleSaveLoanSettings` → rules.update), persisted, and read by G2's enforcement. Design refined post-scout: reuse the EXISTING `loanRulesMaxActiveLoansByMember` (int, live default=1 ⇒ no double loans) for the multi-loan gate — just make it admin-writable — plus two NEW boolean columns for the arrears/penalty gates. No `allowMultipleActiveLoans`/`maxActiveLoansPerMember` columns needed (redundant with the existing int).
  - **DDL APPLIED TO LIVE DB 2026-07-23** (additive, non-destructive; no offline .sql per owner rule; DB `p601229_bank_nkhonde_website`):
    ```sql
    ALTER TABLE group_rules ADD COLUMN requireArrearsClearedBeforeLoan   TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;
    ALTER TABLE group_rules ADD COLUMN requirePenaltiesClearedBeforeLoan TINYINT(1) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0;
    ```
