# ⚠ ARCHITECTURAL PIVOT — decided by the user, cycle 3

**The project is migrating OFF Firebase ONTO PHP + MySQL.** This supersedes the stack section above.

- **Runtime:** PHP + MySQL (a real server process — the static-hosting model is being abandoned).
- **Sequencing:** *Scaffold first, migrate incrementally.* Build the SQL schema, `.env`, and DB connection **alongside** Firebase. Port one feature at a time (auth → groups → loans → payments). **The app keeps working throughout.** Firebase code is deleted only at the very end, once nothing imports it — and only with explicit human sign-off.
- **Local DB:** none yet. The user will stand up MySQL later. This cycle produces **migration files only** — nothing is executed against a live database.

**Why this is not a swap of the data layer:** a browser cannot talk to SQL. Firestore was queryable from client JS because `firestore.rules` enforced auth. MySQL has no equivalent, so every one of the 55 scripts that calls Firestore directly must become a `fetch()` against a PHP endpoint that we write, and `firestore.rules` must be re-implemented as server-side middleware. Auth, storage, and authorization all become ours.

**Implications for the work already done:**
- The cycle-2 hardening of `functions/index.js` is thrown away when Cloud Functions go. It only has value if you deploy it before the migration lands.
- **B2 (rotate the SMTP password) stands regardless of stack.** The credential is compromised and in `git log`. SQL does not change that.
- Firebase items below (BLOCKER-1, BLOCKER-3, firestore.rules reconciliation, indexes) are **PARKED**, not done — they matter only if the migration is abandoned.

## Migration phases (supersede the Firebase phases below)

The old Phase 1–3 backlog predates the pivot and is PARKED (it matters only if the migration is abandoned). The live roadmap is now:

- **M1 — Data layer.** SQL schema + DB config + migration runner. **DONE cycle 3** (`database/migrations/001-003_*.sql`, `config/database.php`, `config/env.php`, `run_migrations.php`).
  - [ ] **M1.1 — Run migrations against the LIVE database. BLOCKED on connection (cycle 5).** Target is now a **live remote MySQL** on a shared cPanel host (`promanaged-it.com`, db `p601229_bank_nkhonde_website`) — not a local server. `php run_migrations.php` fails at connect: `SQLSTATE[HY000] [1045] Access denied for user 'bank_nkhonde_admin'@'109.76.214.5'`. The `.env` parser was verified to pass the password through byte-exact, so this is server-side: the cPanel host has no grant for the developer's public IP (**Remote MySQL** whitelist) and/or the password no longer matches.
    **Needs the human, in cPanel:** (1) rotate the DB password — see B8; (2) add `109.76.214.5` under Remote MySQL; (3) put the new password in `.env`.
    **Agreed procedure once connected:** inspect first — list existing tables + row counts and show the user BEFORE running anything. If the DB is empty, run the migrations. If it already holds tables, STOP and surface them (possible name collision with live data). Migrations are `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, so they are additive and drop nothing.
- **M2 — API + auth tier  *(current)*.** A PHP API that runs *alongside* Firebase. Port features into endpoints in the order **auth → groups → loans → payments**, one vertical at a time. Each endpoint is a `fetch()` target the clients will later call. Scaffoldable without a live DB; validated by `php -l` + static diff review (no test framework).
  - [x] **M2.1 — API foundation + auth vertical. DONE cycle 4, QA PASS.** `api/index.php` (front controller: action router, JSON content-type, top-level `try/catch` masking every Throwable as a generic 500, `display_errors` off), `api/lib/http.php` (`json_response` / `json_error` / `read_json_body`), `api/lib/session.php` (`start_secure_session` with HttpOnly+Secure+SameSite=Lax; `current_user`; `require_auth`; **`require_role($groupId, $allowed)` — re-reads the caller's role from the `members` table server-side, the `firestore.rules` replacement**), `api/lib/password.php` (Argon2id via `password_hash`/`password_verify`), `api/handlers/auth.php` (register / login / logout / session). All queries prepared+bound; the hash is never returned; login failure and unknown-email return an identical generic 401 (no enumeration). Fixed a check-then-insert race in `register()` — the UNIQUE index on `users.email` is now the authority, so a concurrent duplicate signup returns 409 rather than a masked 500.
  - [x] **M2.2 — groups vertical. DONE cycle 4, QA PASS.** `api/handlers/groups.php` + 4 routes registered in `api/index.php`: `groups.mine` (GET), `groups.get` (GET), `groups.create` (POST), `groups.update` (POST). `get_group` allows any member role; `update_group` requires admin/senior_admin. `create_group` writes the group row AND the creator's `senior_admin` membership in **one transaction** — a group with no admin would be unadministrable. `update_group`'s SET clause is assembled from hardcoded literal fragments behind an `array_key_exists` whitelist, so no client key ever reaches the SQL. Creator's `fullName`/`email` for the denormalised members row are read server-side from `users`, not taken from the request body.
  - [x] **M2.3 — members vertical. DONE cycle 4, QA PASS.** `api/handlers/members.php` + 4 routes: `members.list` (GET, any member), `members.add` (POST, admin+), `members.role` (POST, **senior_admin only** — an admin cannot promote anyone, including themselves), `members.status` (POST, admin+). **Last-senior-admin guard** on both mutations: demoting or suspending a group's only active senior_admin returns 409 rather than leaving the group unadministrable. The guard uses `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` inside a transaction, so two concurrent demotions cannot race past the count. `add_member` copies fullName/email server-side from `users` and maps MySQL 1062 to 409 (the PK is the authority, not a check-then-insert).
  - **Assumption recorded (not a user decision):** the status guard counts `role='senior_admin' AND status='active'` — the point is retaining a senior admin who can actually act. Re-activation is always permitted since it only ever adds admins.
  - [x] **M2.4 — loans vertical. DONE cycle 6, QA PASS. Unknown #6 is RESOLVED.**
    **The interest formula was never missing — it was in `scripts/manage_loans.js:1035-1072`, not in any doc.** Ported verbatim to `api/lib/money.php`:
    ```
    reduced_balance, equal principal:
      monthlyPrincipal = principal / period
      balance = principal
      for i in 1..period:
          rate     = [month1, month2, month3][i]     (group_rules; defaults 10/7/5 %, declining)
          interest = round(balance * rate/100, 2)     ← rounded EACH MONTH, then summed
          balance -= monthlyPrincipal                 ← reduced AFTER charging
      totalRepayment = principal + totalInterest
    ```
    `money.php` uses **integer minor units** — zero float arithmetic on currency. Verified: 30000/3mo @10-7-5 → interest **4900.00**, repayable **34900.00** (bit-identical to the live JS). `compute_loan_schedule()` re-parses its own output and throws if the schedule does not sum exactly to the total; the rounding remainder goes to the final instalment, so 1000/3 → 333.33/333.33/**333.34** = 1000.00 exactly. *(The live JS writes 333.33 ×3 = 999.99, a one-tambala shortfall. This fixes it — a deliberate, recorded divergence in the rows; all totals match the JS exactly.)*
    `api/handlers/loans.php` + 5 routes: `loans.list`, `loans.get`, `loans.request`, `loans.approve`, `loans.reject`. **A plain member cannot approve any loan, including their own** (approve/reject gated to admin+), and `list_loans` restricts a member to their own rows **in the SQL**, not in the UI. groupId is always derived from the loan row, never trusted from the request body. Approval writes the loan row + every schedule row in **one transaction** with a `rowCount()` guard against a concurrent double-approval.
    **`flat_rate` returns 501 — not implemented anywhere in the original codebase, and not guessed.** See B10.
  - [x] **M2.6 — Cycle, forced loans, treasurer. DONE cycle 7, QA PASS (one FAIL→fixed).**
    **THE DOMAIN RULE (from the owner):** at cycle end each member is refunded **the interest they personally paid**. That alone would punish light borrowers, so admins **force loans** onto members who are behind, until everyone has borrowed ~equally and paid ~equal interest. `group_rules.loanRulesMinCycleLoanAmount` is the per-cycle borrowing target — this is what that previously-unexplained column is for.
    Migration `004`: `treasurer` added to the role ENUMs; `loans.isForced/forcedBy/forcedReason`; new `cycle_payouts` table; `badges` + `meetings` **dropped** (verified 0 rows, owner signed off); `transactions` kept as the treasurer's cash ledger. **Applied to the live DB.**
    `api/handlers/loans.php` → `loans.force`: an admin/treasurer originates a loan the member never asked for. Same interest engine, same schedule. **Self-dealing guard: an admin cannot force a loan onto themselves (403)** — forcing a loan manufactures an interest obligation that becomes a payout, so one person may not do that to their own account. `loanRulesMaxActiveLoansByMember` is deliberately NOT enforced here (a forced loan IS the override).
    `api/handlers/cycle.php` → `cycle.equity` (who is behind on borrowing, sorted by shortfall), `cycle.payout.preview` (computes, writes nothing), `cycle.settle` (**senior_admin ONLY**, requires `confirm:true`, 409 on double-settle, transactional). Reconciliation invariant asserted in integer minor units in both preview and settle: **Σ payouts must equal the interest pool exactly, or it throws.**
    `treasurer` = admin-equivalent + cash duties; added to every role gate EXCEPT `change_member_role` and `cycle_settle`, which stay senior_admin-only.
    **QA FAIL → FIXED: the cycle aggregates had no date filter**, so they summed the group's *entire history*. On a second cycle every member would have been refunded their first-cycle interest **again** — real money, paid twice. Now windowed to `[cycleDurationStartDate, cycleDurationEndDate)` using `paidAt` (payments, loan_payments) and `approvedAt` (loans). Verified against the live schema.
  - [x] **M2.5 — Penalty engine + loan repayments. DONE cycle 8, QA PASS.**
    **THE PENALTY RULE (from the owner):** a **fixed MWK amount per day**, starting after the grace period, accruing every day **until the debt is cleared**. Not a percentage. Not a one-off.
    **The old code was charging something different from what it told members.** `scripts/group_table.js:153` charged `arrears × rate%` **once**, the instant the due date passed, **ignoring the grace period** — while the overdue notification told members they were accruing *"X% per day"*. Code, schema and member-facing copy all disagreed. The owner's rule is now the truth.
    Migration `005` (applied live): added `group_rules.loanPenaltyDailyAmount` + `contributionPenaltyDailyAmount` as **DECIMAL(15,2)** — the existing `loanPenaltyRate` is `DECIMAL(5,2)` (**max 999.99**) and so *physically could not store* a daily amount of 1,000 MWK or more. Added `'fixed'` to `contributionPenaltyType` (it only allowed `'percentage'`). New `penalty_settlements` ledger.
    `api/lib/penalty.php` → `compute_loan_penalty()`. **Penalties are computed on read, never stored as a running total** — they grow daily, there is no cron, and a stored figure would be stale by morning. Only *settled* penalties (paid/waived) are persisted, and they are netted off so nothing is charged twice. Whole-day `DateTimeImmutable` arithmetic; no float days, no time-of-day drift.
    **Verified end-to-end against the live schema inside a rolled-back transaction:** 500 MWK/day, 5-day grace, instalment due 2026-01-01 → within grace **0**; day one past grace **500.00**; 30 days late **15,000.00**. Nothing persisted.
    `api/handlers/repayments.php` → `repayments.balance`, `repayments.record` (member pays only their own loan; lands `pending`), `repayments.approve` (admin-level; applies the ledger in one transaction), `repayments.waive` (**senior_admin ONLY** — waiving forgives real debt, and is recorded with author + reason, never silently zeroed).
    **Allocation order: penalty → interest → principal.** If principal were cleared first, a penalty could accrue forever on a loan the member is actively paying down. The three portions must sum **exactly** to the amount paid — asserted in code, throws otherwise. Overpayment is rejected 422, never silently credited.
    **A bug in the dispatch brief was caught by the specialist and refused:** the brief said `amountRepaid += amount`, but `amount` includes the penalty, and `remainingBalance = totalRepayment - amountRepaid`. That would have **forgiven principal equal to every penalty paid** — a member paying a 15,000 penalty would have had 15,000 wiped off their loan. Implemented as `amountRepaid += (interest + principal)` with `penaltiesCharged += penalty`, preserving `remainingBalance == SUM(schedule.balance)`.
    Assumption recorded: **one daily charge per loan, not per overdue instalment** ("until it is cleared" reads as one debt, one fee). Also: only one `pending` repayment per loan at a time (409), else two concurrent payments would both be split against a schedule the other is about to change, double-allocating the same interest.
  - [x] **M2.8 — Optional penalty share-out. DONE cycle 9, QA PASS.**
    Migration `006` (live): `group_rules.shareOutPenalties` TINYINT(1) **default 0** (turning it on changes what every member is paid, so no group's payout may change without an explicit opt-in), plus `cycle_payouts.groupPenaltyPool` / `penaltyShare` / `interestRefund` so a settled payout can always be re-explained: *"this is your interest refund, this is your share of the group's penalty income."*
    **Penalties are NOT refunded to whoever paid them.** Refunding a fine to the person fined makes the fine meaningless — being late would cost nothing. Instead the pot is **split EQUALLY across all active members**: the late payer really loses the money and gets back only their 1/N share.
    `payout = interestRefund + (shareOutPenalties ? penaltyPool / activeMembers : 0)`
    The indivisible remainder goes one minor unit at a time to the **earliest joiners** (deterministic, `joinedAt ASC`), so shares sum to the pot exactly. The reconciliation invariant was **tightened**: `SUM(payouts) === interestPool + distributedPenalties`, integer comparison, throws otherwise. A negative pool now throws outright rather than distributing negative payouts.
    **Verified end-to-end against the live schema (rolled-back txn):** interest 100/200/300, penalty pot 1000.00 across 3 members → shares 333.34/333.33/333.33, payouts 433.34/533.33/633.33, **SUM = 1600.00 exactly** (600 interest + 1000 penalties).
  - [x] **M2.7 — Contribution payments. DONE cycle 9, QA PASS. → M2 (API + auth tier) is COMPLETE.**
    `api/handlers/payments.php` + 5 routes: `payments.list` (member sees only their own rows **in the SQL**), `payments.obligations` (the member's "what do I owe" view — seed money, each month's contribution, service fee, each with live penalty), `payments.record`, `payments.approve`, `payments.reject` (both admin-level, `rowCount() !== 1` guard against concurrent adjudication).
    **The app is a ledger, not a payment processor.** A member pays offline, uploads a receipt photo, an admin/treasurer approves. `approvalStatus` is the state machine.
    `totalAmount` is always read from `group_rules` — **never from the client**. A client-supplied "what I owe" is not a fact.
    Contribution penalty = the same fixed-MWK-per-day engine, after grace, computed on read. **Verified live:** 200/day, 5-day grace, due 2026-03-01 → within grace **0**; day one **200.00**; 30 days **6,000.00**; arrears cleared **0**. `percentage` type throws → 501; `fixed` with a NULL daily amount **throws rather than silently charging 0**.
    **Eligibility flags are DERIVED from the ledger, never incremented**, and recomputed inside the approval transaction. Critically: **a pending claim does not make a member current** — otherwise anyone could unlock a loan by uploading a photo of nothing and letting it sit unapproved. Only `approved`/`completed` rows ever count as money.
    Assumptions recorded: `monthlyContributionDayOfMonth` unset → due on the **last** day of the month (an early due date would manufacture penalty days the group never intended); day-31 rules clamp to month length (Feb → 28th, no overflow); one pending claim per obligation at a time (409), which is what makes the reject-rollback reconstruction exact.
  - [x] **M2.10 — Notifications + messages + broadcasts API. DONE cycle 10, QA PASS.** `api/handlers/notifications.php` (list/read/readAll/dismiss) + `messages.php` (list/get/create/reply/update threads + list/create broadcasts). **The inbox boundary has NO admin override:** every one of the 10 notification queries is scoped `WHERE userId = :uid` in SQL — an admin cannot read another member's inbox. Support tickets: a plain member sees only threads they created (`AND createdBy = :uid`); `update_message` (status/priority/assignee) is admin-level with a hardcoded SET-clause whitelist. `create_broadcast` fans out one notification per **active** member in one transaction. `read` and `groups` (both MySQL 8 reserved words) backticked in every query.

- **M3 — Port clients.** 43 scripts (~35,000 lines) import `firebaseConfig.js`. Rewrite them to `fetch()` against the M2 endpoints. Convention: the ported twin is `<original>_sql.js`; the Firebase original stays on disk as the rollback path until cutover.
  - [x] **M3.1 — Shared API client + auth port. DONE cycle 10.** `scripts/api.js` — the SQL-era replacement for the Firebase barrel. Exports `ApiError` (inspectable `.status`/`.body`), `apiGet`, `apiPost`, `login`, `logout`, `getSession`, `requireSession`, `listMyGroups`. Every request funnels through one private `request()` that sets **`credentials: "same-origin"`** — structurally impossible to skip the PHP session cookie. Non-JSON bodies (an HTML 500 page) become a clean `ApiError`, not a confusing `SyntaxError`; a network failure is `ApiError(…, 0)` so callers can tell "offline" from "rejected". `requireSession()` is the replacement for the `onAuthStateChanged` gate every page uses. `scripts/login_sql.js` — port of `login.js`; identical DOM contract, zero Firebase imports, no `innerHTML`. **User enumeration fixed:** the Firebase original showed distinct "Account Not Found" vs "Incorrect Password" (`login.js:136-145`), leaking which emails are registered. Now one generic message.
  - ⚠️ **THE CUTOVER MUST BE ATOMIC — this was nearly a live break.** `login.html` was switched to `login_sql.js` and then **REVERTED**. A SQL login issues a *PHP session cookie*, but all 42 other pages still gate on Firebase `onAuthStateChanged` and 6 scripts read `sessionStorage.isAdmin` (`admin-layout`, `admin_dashboard`, `settings`, `shared-sidebar`, `user_dashboard`, `login`). A user logging in via SQL would have had no Firebase user, been bounced back to login, and looped forever — **the app would have been bricked.** Auth cannot be ported page-by-page; the whole app flips at once, or not at all. `api.js` + `login_sql.js` are on disk, complete and unused, awaiting that flip.
  - [ ] **M3.2 — Port the remaining 42 scripts.** Order: select_group → user_dashboard → admin_dashboard → manage_members → loans → payments → cycle. Each becomes `<name>_sql.js`. Nothing is wired into a page until M4.
    - [x] **File-upload layer — DONE cycle 14, QA PASS. Unblocks the payment/proof ports.** Owner chose a **public `uploads/` web-root folder** (accepted the privacy tradeoff for members' financial receipts; **see B12**). Hardened: `uploads/proofs/.htaccess` disables PHP/CGI execution (mod_php `engine off` + `RemoveHandler` + `FilesMatch … Require all denied`, dual-syntax for Apache 2.2 **and** 2.4) and directory listing; `+ uploads/.htaccess` and an empty `index.html`. `api/lib/uploads.php` — `store_proof_upload()` with the full checklist: `is_uploaded_file` + `move_uploaded_file` only, 5 MB cap, **real MIME sniffed with `finfo`** (client type/extension ignored), `getimagesize` second gate for images, **server-generated random filename** (`random_bytes(16)` + mime-derived extension — the client name never touches the disk path), realpath containment. `api/handlers/files.php` → `files.upload` (multipart; `require_role` on the posted groupId before storing). `.gitignore` ignores uploads but keeps the guards.
      **RCE defense proven, not just read:** a PHP shell renamed `.jpg` is REJECTED by content-sniffing (`getimagesize` fails), and even if it landed, the `.htaccess` disables execution — two independent layers. Added a **fail-closed guard** for a missing `fileinfo` extension (reject, never fall back to the spoofable client type).
      **Client flow to wire at cutover:** upload returns a `url`; the client passes it as `proofOfPaymentImageUrl` into `payments.record` / `repayments.record`. Delete-on-replace helper exists (`delete_proof_upload`) but is not yet wired into those handlers.

Nothing deferred; every feature maps to a real endpoint. `approve_registrations_sql.js`: rebuilt around `invitations.list` (per-email status: pending/accepted/rejected — only the invitee can respond) + an "Invite by Email" form → `invitations.create`. **Toast-deferred: self-registration approve/reject** — there is NO pending-registration state in SQL (`register` creates an active user; joining happens via invite/code), so it would need a `users.status='pending_review'` column + `registrations.list/approve/reject` endpoints.     - [x] **accept_invitation_sql.js — DONE cycle 32, QA PASS (lint clean, zero Firebase, zero innerHTML). Traces to C5.** **MODEL CHANGE:** the Firebase page was a token-in-URL email acceptance that created the account and joined in one step. B1's SQL design has no token column, and `invitations.respond` requires a signed-in caller whose session email matches the invite plus a known invitationId (there is no `invitations.mine` for an invitee to discover it). So this is ported as the **SQL-native join-by-code flow**: `register {email,fullName,password}` → `login` → `codes.redeem {code}` (register does NOT open a session, hence the explicit login). `?code=` prefills the field; an already-signed-in user only enters the code (account fields hidden); a 409 on register falls through to a login attempt so an existing user can still redeem. Errors mapped: 401 wrong password, 409 already-used, 404 invalid/expired (matches the server's no-oracle generic). All messages via textContent. **The email-invite acceptance path would need an `invitations.mine` endpoint — filed as B22 (FUTURE IDEAS); the code path works fully without it.**

      **QA CAUGHT A SILENT ZERO-REPORTING BUG (money-visible).** Both files defined their issued/disbursed loan set as `["disbursed","active","completed"]`. But the `loans.status` enum is `pending|approved|rejected|disbursed|completed|defaulted` — **there is no `'active'`**, and `loans.approve` sets `'approved'` (nothing in the API ever writes `'disbursed'`). So **"total loaned out" and "loans issued" would both have reported ZERO** on a group with live loans — the treasurer's report would have looked balanced while hiding every loan. Corrected to `["approved","disbursed","completed","defaulted"]` in both (defaulted counts: the cash still left the box, and hiding defaults understates what the group is owed). Same fix applied to the interest-income filter.
      **Second silent-empty bug:** the analytics loan trend chart keyed on `l.disbursedAt`, a column the API never populates → the chart would render permanently empty. Now falls back to `approvedAt` (the moment the money is actually committed, since approve+disburse are one call).
      The specialist **refused to port the original's print-window `innerHTML` builder** (it interpolated server-sourced names/amounts into an HTML string — an XSS pattern) and reported it rather than reproducing it. Deferred (no endpoint): PDF/CSV/Excel export, emailing a report, scheduled reports, quarterly/annual granularity. See B19.
    - [x] **settings_sql.js + NEW `api/handlers/profile.php` — DONE cycle 26, QA PASS.** "My account": profile, photo, password, sign out. **Three new endpoints (no migration needed — the `users` table already had every column):** `profile.get`, `profile.update`, `profile.password`.
      **Security posture of profile.php: there is NO uid parameter anywhere in the file** — not a body key, not a query param. Every function operates on the session user, so it is structurally impossible to read or modify someone else's account through it. `passwordHash` is never in the SELECT list. `profile.update` uses a hardcoded literal-fragment whitelist; **`email` is deliberately NOT updatable** (it is the login identity AND the password-reset recipient — changing it needs a verification flow that doesn't exist, and silently allowing it would be an account-takeover path). `profileImageUrl` is refused unless it starts with `/uploads/` (same rule as the rules PDF — no pointing a profile image at a third-party host).
      **`profile.password` requires the CURRENT password** — the Firebase original called `reauthenticateWithCredential()` for exactly this reason: holding a live session must not be enough to change the credential, or an unlocked phone / stolen cookie could lock the real owner out of their money. Wrong current password = a 403, identical whether or not a hash is set.
      Client: sign-out calls `logout()` (destroys the SERVER session — clearing the cookie alone is not a sign-out) and still clears local state + redirects even if that call fails, so a user who clicked "sign out" is never left on a signed-in page.

      **BACKEND FIX THIS CYCLE — `payments.obligations` now accepts an explicit `year`.** It hardcoded `date('Y')`, so a past cycle's arrears were **unaskable** and the specialist had (correctly) pinned the live view to the current year. Now bounded 2000–2100 like `list_payments`' year param, defaulting to the current year (backward-compatible). The contributions page's year selector now drives BOTH the live view and the settled history — previously changing the year silently only reloaded history while the live arrears kept showing "now".
      **`interest_penalties` diverges from the original by necessity:** penalties are a **flat daily MWK amount** now (`loanPenaltyDailyAmount` / `contributionPenaltyDailyAmount`), not a percentage — so the tile and the edit-rates modal show/edit a daily amount. The original's percentage-rate inputs have **no backing column** under the SQL rules schema and were not faked. The modal exposes only fields `rules.update` actually whitelists.

Verified present in the live DB.
      `groups.update` now whitelists the three (admin-gated; empty string clears to NULL — "no rules document" is a real state). **`rulesDocumentUrl` is refused unless it starts with `/uploads/`** — a group's rules document cannot be pointed at an attacker's site. `groups.get` now returns them (any member may READ the rules; only an admin may write them).

Removing a PDF clears the reference but does NOT delete the stored file (deletion is destructive and has no endpoint; an orphaned upload with an unguessable name is harmless, a wrong delete is not). Rules prose rendered via textContent. `view_rules_sql.js` re-pointed from its guessed field names to the real columns.

Zero Firebase code; only innerHTML is two static `&times;`; all admin/member-authored titles+messages via textContent.

Now strips from an unquoted `--` to end of line. (Also recall the earlier fix: it no longer wraps DDL in a fake transaction.)

## POST-COMPLETION MAINTENANCE — 2026-07-14/15 session

The app was declared PROJECT COMPLETE (cycle 47) by a prior session, but had never been smoke-tested against the live database end-to-end. This session ran a full live smoke test (register → login → create group → set rules → upload profile pictures → create members → verify) and a dashboard UX audit, both requested by the owner. Found and fixed 3 real, previously-invisible production bugs, plus dashboard UX gaps. All fixes QA-reviewed and verified live against the production database before being recorded here.

### Bugs found and fixed

1. **`groups.create` never created a `group_rules` row — every group ever created was permanently unable to have its money rules configured.** `rules.update` only ever `UPDATE`s an existing row; nothing ever `INSERT`ed one. Confirmed live: the owner's own real "Test" group (created 2026-07-14) has 0 `group_rules` rows and is still broken — **needs a manual backfill** (an admin calling `rules.update` on it will 404 until someone runs one `INSERT INTO group_rules (groupId, cycleDurationStartDate, loanPenaltyType, contributionPenaltyType) VALUES ('<that groupId>', NOW(), 'fixed', 'fixed')` against it). Fixed in `api/handlers/groups.php`'s `create_group()`: inserts a `group_rules` row in the same transaction as the group/member insert, using only server-controlled values (schema defaults for everything except `cycleDurationStartDate` and, per bug 2 below, `loanPenaltyType`/`contributionPenaltyType`). QA PASS. Verified live: a freshly created group now has a working `group_rules` row immediately.

2. **`loanPenaltyType`/`contributionPenaltyType` were not in `rules.update`'s whitelist at all, and the schema default (`'percentage'`) is a mode the penalty engine never implemented** (`api/lib/penalty.php` and the inline contribution-penalty logic in `api/handlers/payments.php` both throw/501 on `'percentage'`, only `'fixed'` is real). This meant every group's penalty computation was broken by default with no way to fix it via the app. Fixed: both columns added to `update_rules()`'s whitelist (validated against the real enum values), and `create_group()`'s new `group_rules` insert explicitly sets both to `'fixed'` so new groups are born in a working state. QA PASS. Verified live: a fresh group defaults to `'fixed'`/`'fixed'`, and `rules.update` can now set either value explicitly.

3. **`profile.update` (self-service) never synced the denormalized `fullName`/`phone`/`whatsappNumber`/`profileImageUrl` copies in `members`.** A member changing their own profile picture/phone/name via Settings would update `users` correctly but every group roster (`members.list` — admin dashboards, manage_members, contacts, etc.) kept showing the stale old value forever. Fixed in `api/handlers/profile.php`'s `update_profile()`: now also updates every `members` row for that uid (all group memberships, `email` deliberately excluded, each field gated by `array_key_exists` so untouched fields aren't blasted), wrapped in the same transaction as the `users` update. QA PASS. Verified live: updating phone via `profile.update` now shows correctly in `members.list` immediately.

### App-wide latent bug found and fixed (via ui-designer, during dashboard polish)

4. **Colored toasts (`success`/`warning`/`danger`/`info`) have silently rendered as plain grey app-wide since the app was built.** `showToast(msg, type)` sets `className = "toast toast-${type}"` (hyphenated), but `design-system.css` and `admin-layout.css` had color rules written as `.toast.danger` etc. (space-separated, no hyphen) — a selector that never matched. Fixed: renamed the CSS selectors to match what the JS actually emits (`.toast.toast-success`, `.toast.toast-danger`, etc.) in both files. **One remaining occurrence of the old broken pattern in `styles/mobile-design-system.css` — confirmed dead/unloaded by any live page, not fixed (out of scope, zero live impact).** QA PASS.

### Dashboard UX fixes (admin_dashboard, user_dashboard)

- **`user_dashboard_sql.js`** — `uploadPaymentBtn` was a dead-end toast; now routes to `loan_payments.html` (confirmed real working `files.upload` flow there). `requestLoanBtn` was also a dead-end toast; **investigated and confirmed no member-facing "request a new loan" flow exists anywhere in the ported app** (only admin-initiated `loans.force` and admin approve/reject exist) — reworded the toast to be honest about this instead of vague, and this gap is now recorded below as a real missing feature, not silently left as a mystery dead button.
- Both `admin_dashboard_sql.js` and `user_dashboard_sql.js`: `safeGet()` was silently swallowing non-auth fetch failures and rendering them identically to "zero activity" — an admin/member with a failed network request would see a dashboard that looked like a clean, empty, healthy account. Now surfaces one toast per failed load (not spammed per section).
- `user_dashboard.html`'s `#spinner` element existed in markup but was never wired up (dead code, page popped from blank to loaded with no transition). Now wired to match the working pattern already used on `admin_dashboard_sql.js`.
- `admin_dashboard_sql.js`'s Collection Trends section: the Member Participation pie chart was gated only on member count, so it rendered alone (orphaned, 100%-inactive) whenever a group had zero real financial activity. Now gated on financial activity too, so a genuinely inactive group shows one consistent empty state instead of one lone chart.
- Fixed as a side effect: toast close buttons now have `aria-label="Dismiss notification"` (were unlabeled icon-only buttons).

All dashboard fixes QA-passed (13-point review, app-wide toast-selector precision independently re-verified).

### New gaps recorded (not built — no product decision made, not silently invented)

- **No member-facing "request a new loan" flow exists.** Only admins can originate loans (`loans.force`) or approve/reject member-submitted ones — but nothing lets a member submit a NEW loan REQUEST themselves via `loans.request`, even though that endpoint exists and is live. `requestLoanBtn` on the user dashboard is currently an honest dead-end. **Needs an owner decision**: is member-initiated loan requesting in scope, or is "loans only originate from an admin/forced" the intended product model?
- **The owner's own real "Test" group (groupId starts `65a4e2e1...`, created 2026-07-14) still has 0 `group_rules` rows** — it predates today's `create_group()` fix and needs a one-time manual backfill (see bug 1 above) before its rules can ever be set.
